Multivariate functorial difference
Robert Paré

Abstract
Partial difference operators for a large class of functors between presheaf categories are introduced, extending our previous work on the difference operator to the multivariable case. These combine into the Jacobian profunctor which provides the setting for a lax chain rule. We introduce a functorial version of multivariable Newton series whose aim is to recover a functor from its iterated differences. Not all functors are recovered but we get a best approximation in the form of a left adjoint, and the induced comonad is idempotent. Its fixed points are what we call soft analytic functors, a generalization of the well studied multivariable analytic functors.

In memory of Phil Scott, 1947–2023

Philip Scott

I knew Phil for most of his career, from when he was a
post-doctoral fellow at McGill in 1977, a colleague the following
year at Dalhousie, and a friend ever since. His knowledge
of the literature in category theory, logic and computer
science was phenomenal. He travelled a lot and spoke
to many people. This way, he kept up to date on the latest
developments and each time he visited Halifax, he had
some new topic he thought I should look at. This was
good advice which I wish I had been more diligent
following up. We’ve lost a great ambassador for our
subject as well as a friend. I dedicate this work to him.

Introduction

This is a sequel to
(
Paré,, 2024
)
. Here we are interested in
the structure of functors
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

(
\bf A
and
\bf B
small categories)
generalizing the difference calculus for endofunctors of

{\bf Set}
. An important example is given by the generalized
analytic functors of
Fiore et al., (2008)
. As in that work,
profunctors are central. That is perhaps the main difference
the present work has with
(
Paré,, 2024
)
. This is somewhat of
a simplification like saying that multivariate calculus is just
single variable calculus plus linear algebra. The added
dimensions open up a whole array of possibilities.

The work here is a categorified version of the classical
partial difference operators for real functions

{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}

a discrete version of partial derivatives. The analogy is
quite fruitful.

As the paper is quite long, it may be helpful to point out the
main results, namely the lax chain rule (Theorem
4.2
)
and the Newton adjunction (Theorem
5.1
)
together with the convergence theorem (Theorem
5.2
).
These results are proper to the categorical setting and
have no counterpart for real-valued functions. They could not
be formulated without the pivotal definitions of the (discrete)
Jacobian as a profunctor (Definition
4.1
)
and soft analytic functor (Definition
5.2
).

Apart from the obvious
(
Fiore et al.,, 2008
)
and the
references therein, the present work was strongly influenced
by the work of the Calgary-Ottawa-Montreal consortium on
tangent categories and cartesian differential categories.
Several talks in the ATCAT seminar by regulars Geoff
Cruttwell and Marcello Lanfranchi as well as guest speakers,
notably Robin Cockett and JS Lemay, helped form my
ideas on the categorical theory of differentials. After
completion of this work, the paper “Cartesian difference categories”
by
Alvarez-Picallo and Pacaud Lemay, (2021)
came to my attention.
This is clearly relevant as it deals with the categorical
understanding of finite difference. What is less clear
is precisely how they are related. Further work in this
direction should prove fruitful.

Thanks to Nathanael Arkor, Andreas Blass, John Bourke,
Aaron Fairbanks, Marcelo Fiore, Richard Garner,
Theo Johnson-Freyd, Tom Leinster, Matías Menni, Deni
Salja, and Peter Selinger for their insightful comments
and interest. A special thanks to Peter Selinger for helping
me prepare the final version in the MSC style.

1
Profunctors

Profunctors (a.k.a. bimodules, modules, distributors) will be
at the heart of this work. Widely viewed as categorified relations,
for our purposes they are better viewed as categorified matrices.
They correspond to cocontinuous functors between functor
categories. Such functors are considered to be linear. This section
contains nothing new (except perhaps Definition
1.2
and
Proposition
1.2
). It is included for completeness and
to set notation.

1.1
Definitions

We have opted, not without thought, for the following
definition which is the opposite of the majority view.

Definition 1.1
.

(Lawvere, Bénabou)

Let
{\bf A}
and
{\bf B}
be small categories. A

profunctor

P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
is
a functor
P\colon{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
. A

morphism of profunctors

t\colon P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Q
is a
natural transformation.

This gives the basic data for a bicategory,
{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}
,
of profunctors. Composition is given by “matrix multiplication”
which takes the form of a coend. For
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
and
Q\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
, the
composite
Q\otimes P
is defined by

Q\otimes P(A,C)=\int^{B\in{\bf B}}Q(B,C)\times P(A,B)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The identity
\Id_{\bf A}\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}
is the
hom functor

\Id_{\bf A}(A,A^{\prime})={\bf A}(A,A^{\prime})\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The reader is referred to the standard texts (see e.g.
Borceaux, 1994b ()
) for a proof
that we do get a bicategory.

For explicit computations involving profunctors, the
following notation is useful. An element
x\in P(A,B)

is denoted by a pointed arrow, sometimes called a
heteromorphism,
x\colon A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B
, or

x\colon A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.91615pt\raise-5.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B
if it’s necessary to keep track
of the profunctor. The functoriality of
P
manifests itself
as a composition

\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{xf}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{gx}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{g}

which is associative (left, right, and middle) and unitary.

It is in dealing with composition that this is most useful. An
element of
Q\otimes P(A,C)
is an equivalence class of
pairs

if we have

\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{x^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{B}

\textstyle{B}

\scriptstyle{y}

\textstyle{C\hbox to0.0pt{\ ,\hss}}

so they are equivalent iff there exists a path of pairs

\textstyle{A}

\scriptstyle{y}

(*)

We write the equivalence class
[A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise as

y\otimes_{B}x\mbox{\ \ or simply\ \ }y\otimes x\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The equivalence relation is generated by

yb\otimes x=y\otimes bx\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Every functor
F\colon{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf B}
induces two
profunctors

F_{*}\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}\quad\quad F^{*}\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}

and

F_{*}(A,B)={\bf B}(FA,B)\quad\quad F^{*}(B,A)={\bf B}(B,FA).

F^{*}
is right adjoint to
F_{*}
in
{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}
.

1.2
Biclosedness

The bicategory
{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}
is biclosed, that is
\otimes

admits right adjoints in each variable giving two hom profunctors

\oslash
and
\obslash
characterized by natural
bijections

P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Q\obslash_{\bf C}R

Q\otimes_{\bf B}P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>R

Q\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>R\oslash_{\bf A}P

for profunctors

\textstyle{\bf A}
\textstyle{\bf B}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{\bf A}
\textstyle{{\bf C}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\bf B}
\textstyle{{\bf C}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{Q}

We use Lambek’s notation for the internal homs. Inasmuch
as
\otimes
is a product, the right adjoints are quotients
of a sort.

An element of
(Q\obslash_{\bf C}R)(A,B)
is a

{\bf C}
-natural transformation

t\colon Q(B,-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>R(A,-)

and an element of
(R\oslash_{\bf A}P)(B,C)
is an

{\bf A}
-natural transformation

u\colon P(-,B)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>R(-,C)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

1.3
Cocontinuous functors

Our interest is in functors between functor categories and a
profunctor will produce an adjoint pair of them. A profunctor

\colon{\bf 1}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}
is a functor

{\bf 1}^{op}\times{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}

which we identify with a functor
\Phi\colon{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
.
A profunctor
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
will then produce,
by composition, a functor

P\otimes_{\bf A}(\ \ )\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

with a right adjoint

P\obslash_{\bf B}(\ \ )\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

It follows that
P\otimes_{\bf A}(\ \ )
is cocontinuous and is
considered to be the linear functor corresponding to the matrix

P
.

As is well-known, we have:

Proposition 1.1
.

The following categories are equivalent:

(1)

Profunctors
{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}

(2)

Cocontinuous functors
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

(3)

Adjoint pairs

\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}

Given a cocontinuous functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
, the corresponding profunctor
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
is given by

P(A,B)=F({\bf A}(A,-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Note that this doesn’t use cocontinuity of
F
, which leads
to the following.

Definition 1.2
.

The
core
of a functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
is the profunctor defined by

\mbox{Cor}(F)(A,B)=F({\bf A}(A,-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The functor

\mbox{Cor}(F)\otimes(\ \ )\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

is the “linear core” of
F
.

Proposition 1.2
.

Cor
is right adjoint to the functor

{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}({\bf A},{\bf B})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}C}\!{\it at}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})

which takes a profunctor
P
to the (cocontinuous) functor

P\otimes_{\bf A}(\ \ )
.

Proof.

A profunctor
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
can be viewed,
by exponential adjointness, as a functor
{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
. Then
Cor
is just restriction along
the Yoneda embedding

F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\quad\longmapsto\quad{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48265pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.69412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.38824pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47023pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.5442pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.08841pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

and
P\otimes_{\bf A}(\ \ )
is left Kan extension

\textstyle{{\bf A}^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{{\bf A}^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{Y}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{\mbox{Lan}_{Y}P=P\otimes(\ \ )}
.

∎

Thus for
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
,

\mbox{Cor}(F)\otimes_{A}(\ \ )
is the best approximation
to
F
by a cocontinuous functor. As a matter of interest,
the counit of the adjunction

\epsilon(F)\colon\mbox{Cor}(F)\otimes(\ \ )\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F

is given as follows. An element of
(\mbox{Cor}(F)\otimes\Phi)(B)
is an equivalence class

[x\in\Phi A,y\colon A\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.60873pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.60873pt\raise-6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mbox{\scriptsize Cor}(F)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces B]_{A}

so

[{\bf A}(A,-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.4945pt\raise 5.83888pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bar{x}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.98901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi,\ y\in F({\bf A}(A,-))(B)]

giving

F({\bf A}(A,-))(B)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47214pt\raise 6.71388pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.96388pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F({\bar{x}})(B)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.48764pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.97528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F(\Phi)(B)

y\longmapsto F({\bar{x}})(B)(y)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Example 1.1
.

If
{\bf A}
and
{\bf B}
are discrete categories, i.e. sets

A
and
B
, then a profunctor
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}

is just a
A\times B
-matrix of sets
[P_{ab}]
and a morphism
of profunctors
P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>P^{\prime}
a
A\times B
-matrix of functions. The
identity
\Id_{\bf A}
is the matrix with
1
’s on the diagonal
and
0
elsewhere. If
{\bf C}
is another discrete category
and
Q\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
a profunctor, then

Q\otimes_{\bf B}P
is the
B\times C
-matrix

\Bigl[\sum_{b\in B}Q_{bc}\times P_{ab}\Bigr]_{\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}

If
R\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
then

R\oslash_{\bf A}P=\Bigl[\prod_{a\in A}R_{ac}^{P_{ab}}\Bigr]

and

Q\obslash_{\bf C}R=\Bigl[\prod_{c\in C}R_{ac}^{Q_{bc}}\Bigr]_{\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}

A profunctor
X\colon{\bf 1}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}
is a
1\times A
matrix
of sets, i.e. a vector
[X_{a}]
and
P\otimes_{\bf A}X
is the vector

\Bigl[\sum_{a\in A}P_{ab}\times X_{a}\Bigr]_{b\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}

On the other hand for
Y\colon{\bf 1}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
a

B
-vector
P\obslash_{\bf B}Y

\Bigl[\prod_{b}Y_{b}^{P_{ab}}\Bigr]_{a\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}

So
P\otimes_{\bf A}(\ \ )
is a “linear” functor, and

P\obslash_{\bf B}(\ \ )
a “monomial” functor.

2
Tense functors

In
Paré, (2024)
we developed a difference calculus for taut endofunctors
of
{\bf Set}
, functors preserving inverse images. However, the important
example of multivariable analytic functors of
Fiore et al., (2008)
are not taut. In
fact the linear functors
P\otimes(\ \ )
are not taut. They don’t even
preserve monos. What we need are functors preserving complemented
subobjects and their inverse images. Of course, in
{\bf Set}
, all
subobjects are complemented so it would make no difference, so maybe
that’s what taut should be after all. But the word “taut” is pretty well established,
so we use “tense” instead.

2.1
Complemented subobjects

In this section we collect some useful facts about complemented
subobjects in functor categories
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
, most of which are
well-known from topos theory. We first list some general topos theory
results which will be useful for us. Proofs can be found in any of
the standard topos theory books (see
Borceaux, 1994a ()
for an easily
accessible account).

Definition 2.1
.

A subobject
\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.49997pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 8.99994pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
is
complemented
if there exists
another subobject
\Psi^{\prime}\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
for which the induced
morphishm

Proposition 2.1
.

1. A subobject
\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.59993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.19987pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
is complemented iff its characteristic

1+1

\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{1+1}
\textstyle{\Phi}

\scriptstyle{\chi_{\Psi}}
\textstyle{\Omega}
\textstyle{1+1}
\scriptstyle{\langle\mbox{true, false}\rangle}

2. Complemented subobjects are closed under composition.

3. Complemented objects are stable under pullback: if

\Psi\hookrightarrow\Phi
is complemented and

f\colon\Theta\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
, then
\neg f^{-1}(\Psi)=f^{-1}(\neg\Psi)
and we have isomorphisms

\textstyle{\Psi+(\neg\Psi)}
\textstyle{\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{f^{-1}(\Psi)+f^{-1}(\neg\Psi)}
\textstyle{\Psi+(\neg\Psi)}
\scriptstyle{g+g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{f^{-1}(\Psi)+f^{-1}(\neg\Psi)}
\textstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{f}

4. If
\Psi\hookrightarrow\Phi
is complemented, its complement
is
\neg\Psi
, so complements are unique when they exist.

5. Given an inverse image diagram (pullback)

\textstyle{\Phi\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}}

\textstyle{\Gamma}

\textstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\Phi\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}}
\scriptstyle{f}
Pb

f
restricts to

\textstyle{\neg\Psi}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\neg\Gamma}
\textstyle{\neg\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\neg g}
\textstyle{\neg\Gamma}
\textstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{f}

and the resulting square is also a pullback.

is always complemented for any subobject

Proposition 2.2
.

For

we have

(1)

This is equivalent to saying that for all

x\ \ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>x^{\prime}

(3) For any
\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.59993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.19987pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
,
\neg\Psi
is
complemented and its complement is
\neg\neg\Psi

which is the smallest complemented subobject of

\Phi
containing
\Psi
.

Thus the class of complemented subobjects consists of
all transformations right orthogonal to the representable
transformations
{\bf A}(f,-)
, suggesting that it may
be the
{\mathcal{}M}
part of a factorization system on

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
, which is indeed the case.

For
\Phi
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
, let
\sim
be the
equivalence relation on the set of all elements of
\Phi

generated by identifying
x\in\Phi A
with

\Phi f(x)\in\Phi A^{\prime}
for all
f\colon A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}
. Thus

x\in\Phi A\sim x^{\prime}\in\Phi A^{\prime}
if there exists a zigzag
path as in (2) above.
The set of equivalence classes is the set of components
of
\Phi
,
\pi_{0}\Phi=\varinjlim_{A}\Phi A
, and two
elements are equivalent if and only if they are in the
same component.

Definition 2.2
.

A transformation
t\colon\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
is

\pi_{0}
-
surjective
if
\pi_{0}t\colon\pi_{0}\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\pi_{0}\Phi
is surjective.

Thus
t
is
\pi_{0}
-surjective iff every element of

\Phi
is connected by a zigzag path to an element
in the image of
t
.

Proposition 2.3
.

(1)

t,u\ \pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}\Rightarrow tu\ \pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
.

(2)

tu\ \pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}\Rightarrow t\ \pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
.

(3)

Every
t
factors uniquely up to a unique
isomorphism as a
\pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
followed
by a complemented monomorphism.

(4)

The
\pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
transformations
are left orthogonal to the complemented monos.

Proof.

(1) and (2) are obvious from the definition. For (3), let

t\colon\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
be any transformation. Let

\Phi_{0}A\subseteq\Phi A
be the set of all

x\in\Phi A
connected to an element in the image
of
t
.
\Phi_{0}
is easily seen to be a complemented
subfunctor of
\Phi
, and is in fact the union of all
of the components of
\Phi
that contain an element
in the image of
t
. Then
t
factors as

\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.0313pt\raise 5.65279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi_{0}\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.98901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi

and
t_{0}
is
\pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
by construction.
This is our factorization. The uniqueness part will follow
from (4).

Consider a commutative square in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

\textstyle{\ \Gamma\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta\ }
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\ \Gamma\ }
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\ \Delta\ }
\scriptstyle{s}

where
t
is
\pi_{0}\mbox{-surjective}
and
m
is
a complemented mono. Any
x\in\Phi A
is connected
to some
t(A^{\prime})(y)
for
y\in\Psi A^{\prime}
, so
s(A)(x)

is connected to
s(A^{\prime})t(A^{\prime})(y)=m(A^{\prime})r(A^{\prime})(y)
. As

m
is complemented, this implies that
s(A)(x)

is in
\Gamma(A)
. This gives the diagonal fill-in

\delta\colon\Phi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Gamma
such that
m\ \delta=s

and
\delta\ t=r
.
\delta
is unique as
m
is monic.
∎

These results tell us that we have a factorization system on

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
with
{\mathcal{}E}
the class of

\pi_{0}
-surjections and
{\mathcal{}M}
the class of
complemented monos. We call it the
Boolean factorization
.
Note that the class of
\pi_{0}
-surjections is not stable
under pullback however. Consider morphisms

f_{i}\colon A_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A_{i},i=1,2
in
{\bf A}
and
consider the pullback

\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{2},-)}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{0},-)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{{\bf A}(f_{2},-)}
\textstyle{\Sigma}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{2},-)}
\textstyle{\Sigma}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{1},-)}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{1},-)}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{0},-)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{{\bf A}(f_{1},-)}
Pb

\Sigma(A)
consists of pairs of morphisms
(g_{1},g_{2})

such that

\textstyle{A_{2}}
\textstyle{A}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}
\textstyle{A_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{A_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{1}}
\textstyle{A}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}

commutes, which well may be empty for all
A
. In that
case, taking
\pi_{0}
of the above pullback gives

\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{1}

showing that
{\bf A}(g_{1},-)
is
\pi_{0}
-surjective
but its pullback is not.

Nevertheless, it will be useful for us in Section
5

where we will be particularly interested in transformations
defined on sums of representables. We record here the
following facts for use later.

A natural transformation

t\colon\sum_{j\in J}{\bf A}(C_{j},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum_{i\in I}{\bf A}(A_{i},-)

is determined by a function on the indices
\alpha\colon J\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>I
and a
J
-family of functions
\langle f_{j}\rangle
,

f_{j}\colon A_{\alpha(j)}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>C_{j}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Write
t=\displaystyle{\sum_{\alpha}}{\bf A}(f_{j},-)
.

Proposition 2.4
.

With
t
,
\alpha
,
f_{i}
as above we have

(1)

t
is a complemented mono if and only if

\alpha
is one-to-one and the
f_{j}
are isomorphims.

(2)

t
is
\pi_{0}
-surjective if and only if

\alpha
is onto.

(3)

For a general
t
given by
(\alpha,\langle f_{j}\rangle)
we get its Boolean factorization
by factoring
\alpha

\textstyle{J}
\textstyle{K}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{J}
\textstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{K}
\scriptstyle{\mu}

and then taking

\sum_{j\in J}{\bf A}(C_{j},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.28284pt\raise 9.12505pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.87505pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{k},-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.78473pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.56946pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum_{k\in K}{\bf A}(A_{k},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.30574pt\raise 9.12505pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.87505pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sum_{\mu}{\bf A}(1_{A_{i}},-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.78363pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.56726pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum_{i\in I}{\bf A},(A_{i},-)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

It’s implicit in (1), but may be worth mentioning explicitly,
that the complemented subobjects of

\sum_{i\in I}{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
are the subsums, i.e. of the
form
\sum_{k\in K}{\bf A}(A_{k},-)
for
K\subseteq I
.
It is also clear from the fact that each hom functor

{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
is connected and complemented, so is one
of the components of
\sum_{i\in I}{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
, and
any complemented subfunctor is a union of components.

The following is well-known (see
Borceaux, 1994a ()
, Example 7.2.4).

Proposition 2.5
.

Every subobject in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
is complemented
(
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
is boolean) if and only if
{\bf A}
is
a groupoid.

We end this subsection with the following, which says that
limits and confluent colimits of complemented subobjects
are again complemented.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
\Gamma\colon{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
be a
diagram in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
and
\Gamma_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.59993pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 9.19987pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Gamma

a subdiagram such that for every
I
,
\Gamma_{0}(I)\hookrightarrow\Gamma(I)
is complemented, then

(1)
\varprojlim\Gamma_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\varprojlim\Gamma
is a complemented
subobject.

If
{\bf I}
is confluent we also have that

(2)
\varinjlim\Gamma_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\varinjlim\Gamma
is a complemented
subobject.

Proof.

(1)
\Gamma_{0}(I)\hookrightarrow\Gamma(I)
is complemented
iff for every
f\colon A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}
,

\textstyle{\Gamma_{0}(I)(A^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I)(A^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{0}(I)(A)}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{0}(I)(A^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{0}(I)(f)}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{0}(I)(A)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I)(A)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I)(A)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I)(A^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(I)(f)}

is a pullback (
2.2
(1)). Limits of pullback
diagrams are pullbacks, and the result follows.

(2) Recall from
Paré, (2024)
that a category
{\bf I}
is
confluent if any span can be completed to a commutative
square, and that confluent colimits commute with inverse
image diagrams in
{\bf Set}
. This gives (2) immediately.
∎

Corollary 2.1
.

The intersection of an arbitrary family of complemented
subobjects in a presheaf category is again complemented.
The same for union.

Proof.

Let
\Psi_{i}\hookrightarrow\Phi
be a family of
complemented subobjects. Without loss of generality
we can assume that the total subobject

\Phi\hookrightarrow\Phi
is contained in it so that
the indexing poset
{\bf I}
is connected. Then by the
previous proposition

\varprojlim\Psi_{i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\varprojlim\Phi

is a complemented mono. Because
{\bf I}
is
connected the limit of the constant diagram

\varprojlim\Phi\cong\Phi
, and the
\varprojlim\Psi_{i}

is
\cap\Psi_{i}\hookrightarrow\Phi
. The lattice
of complemented subobjects of
\Phi
is
self-dual which implies the result for unions.
∎

Note that this result does not hold in an arbitrary
Grothendieck topos.

2.2
Tense functors

As mentioned above, the functors

P\otimes(\ \ )\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

arising from profunctors are not generally taut. In fact
they don’t even preserve monos in general. This may not
be surprising if we consider the tensor product of modules
but one might have hoped that things would be better in
the simpler
{\bf Set}
case.

Example 2.1
.

For any epimorphism
e\colon A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}
in
{\bf A}
,
the natural transformation
{\bf A}(e,-)\colon{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf A}(A,-)
is a monomorphism. If, for
a profunctor
