2.5
Multivariable analytic functors

Following
Fiore et al., (2008)
we define
analytic functors of several variables
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
as follows. First,
for a category
{\bf A}
, its
exponential

!{\bf A}
(from linear logic) is the free
symmetric strict monoidal category generated by
{\bf A}
.
In concrete terms,
!{\bf A}
is the category with
objects finite sequences
\langle A_{1}\dots A_{n}\rangle

of objects of
{\bf A}
and morphisms finite
sequences of morphisms of
{\bf A}
controlled by
a permutation. There are no morphisms between
sequences unless they have the same length and then

\langle A_{1}\dots A_{n}\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\langle A^{\prime}_{1}\dots,A^{\prime}_{n}\rangle

is a permutation of the indices,
\sigma\in S_{n}
and a
sequence of morphisms

f_{i}\colon A_{\sigma i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}_{i}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Composition is as expected

(\tau,\langle g_{i}\rangle)(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)=(\sigma\tau,\langle g_{i}f_{\tau i}\rangle).

An
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}

symmetric sequence
is a
profunctor
P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
, which for us is a
functor
(!{\bf A})^{op}\times{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
.
(
Warning
: Our definition of profunctor is the
opposite of theirs.)
P
encodes what are to be the
coefficients of a
{\bf B}
-family of multivariable
power series.

The
analytic functor
determined by
P

\widetilde{P}\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

is given by

\widetilde{P}(\Phi)(B)=\int^{\langle A_{1}\dots A_{n}\rangle\in\,!{\bf A}}P(A_{1}\dots A_{n};B)\times\Phi A_{1}\times\Phi A_{2}\times\dots\times\Phi A_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

We’ll show that
\widetilde{P}
is tense. Define a
profunctor
Q\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}
by

Q(A_{1},\dots,A_{n};A)={\bf A}(A_{1},A)+{\bf A}(A_{2},A)+\dots+{\bf A}(A_{n},A)

with the obvious definition on morphisms. We may consider

Q
as a functor
(!{\bf A})^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
and

\widetilde{P}
is the left Kan extension of
P
, considered
as a functor
(!{\bf A})^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
, along
Q

\textstyle{(!{\bf A})^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{(!{\bf A})^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{P}={\rm Lan}_{Q}P}

For our purposes a different description of
\widetilde{P}

will be useful.

Proposition 2.13
.

1.
\widetilde{P}
is the composite
P\otimes(Q\obslash(\ \ ))

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.2411pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Q\obslash(\ \ )}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.23723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.47446pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{!{\bf A}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.70618pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P\otimes(\ \ )}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.13728pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.27457pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

2.
Q
satisfies the condition of Proposition
2.12
.

Proof.

(1) Let
\Phi\in{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
. An element of

(Q\obslash\Phi)(A_{1},\dots,A_{n})
is a natural
transformation

{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\dots+{\bf A}(A_{n},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi

which by the universal property of coproduct and the
Yoneda lemma corresponds to an element of

\Phi A_{1}\times\Phi A_{2}\times\dots\times\Phi A_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Now the result follows by the definition of
P\otimes(\ \ )

and
\widetilde{P}
.

(2)
Q(A_{1},\dots,A_{n};-)={\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\dots+{\bf A}(A_{n},-)
a sum of
representables each of which is connected. So

\pi_{0}Q(A_{1},\dots,A_{n};-)\cong n

and, as
!{\bf A}
has only morphisms between
sequences of the same length, we get

\pi_{0}Q(A_{1},\dots,A_{n};-)\cong\pi_{0}Q(A^{\prime}_{1},\dots,A^{\prime}_{n};-)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

∎

Corollary 2.3
.

\widetilde{P}
is tense.

Corollary 2.4
.

For
P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
an
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}

symmetric sequence and
R\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
a
profunctor, we have

\widetilde{R\otimes P}\cong R\otimes\widetilde{P}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\widetilde{R\otimes P}&\cong&(R\otimes P)\otimes(Q\obslash(\ ))\\
&\cong&R\otimes(P\otimes(Q\obslash(\ ))\\
&\cong&R\otimes\widetilde{P}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}\end{array}

∎

3
Partial difference operators

We want to think of a functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
as a
{\bf B}
-family of
{\bf Set}
-valued
functors in
{\bf A}
-variables and study its change under
small perturbations of the variables. The context is that of
tense functors and for these we get a theory that parallels the
usual calculus of differences for real-valued functions of
several variables, much as our theory for taut functors did
for single variables
(
Paré,, 2024
)
.

3.1
Partial difference

A functor
\Phi\in{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
is a multisorted algebra,
the sorts being the objects of
{\bf A}
, with unary operations
corresponding to the morphisms of
{\bf A}
. Freely adding
a single element of sort
A
gives

\Phi\leadsto\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Definition 3.1
.

The
A
-shift
functor, for an object
A
in
{\bf A}

is

S_{A}\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

S_{A}(\Phi)=\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

S_{A}
is clearly tense, in fact a tense monad. Although
we won’t use it here, it may be of interest to note that an
Eilenberg-Moore algebra for
S_{A}
consists of a functor

\Phi\in{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
together with an element

x\in\Phi A
. A Kleisli morphism
\Phi\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}\Psi

is a partial natural transformation

\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\Psi}
\textstyle{\Phi_{0}}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\Phi_{0}}
\textstyle{\Psi}

defined on a complemented subobject
\Phi_{0}
together
with a transformation on the complement

\Phi^{\prime}_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf A}(A,-)
, perhaps quantifying the degree
of undefinedness.

These monads commute with each other

S_{A_{1}}\circ S_{A_{2}}\cong S_{A_{2}}\circ S_{A_{1}}

and for every
f\colon A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}
there is a monad morphism

S_{A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>S_{A^{\prime}}
which is tense.

The main definition of the paper is the following.

Definition 3.2
.

The
partial difference with respect to

A
, or the

A
-
partial difference
,
\Delta_{A}[F]
, of a tense
functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

is given by

\Delta_{A}[F]\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)=F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\setminus F(\Phi)\,,

the complement of
F(\Phi)\hookrightarrow F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))
.

Proposition 3.1
.

For a tense functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
,

\Delta_{A}[F]
is also a tense functor. A tense natural
transformation
t\colon F\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>G
restricts to one,

\Delta_{A}[t]\colon\Delta_{A}[F]\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta_{A}[G]
, making

\Delta_{A}
a functor

\Delta_{A}\colon{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})\,.

Proof.

Let
\phi\colon\Psi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
be a natural transformation. We have
the following pullbacks

\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)}
\textstyle{\ \Psi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\ \Psi\ }
\textstyle{\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-)}
\textstyle{\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-)}
\textstyle{\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)}
\scriptstyle{\phi+{\bf A}(A,-)}
Pb
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\textstyle{\ \ F\Psi\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\scriptstyle{F\phi}
\textstyle{\ \ F\Psi\ }
\textstyle{F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{F(\phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
Pb

From the second one we get that
F(\phi+{\bf A}(A,-))
restricts
to the complements and gives another pullback

\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\phi)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\scriptstyle{F(\phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
Pb

which gives functoriality and tenseness.

Suppose
t\colon F\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>G
is a tense transformation. Then
we get a pullback for any
\Phi

\textstyle{\ G(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Phi)\ }
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
Pb

so
t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))
restricts to the complements,
giving another pullback

\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}t{]}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
Pb

It follows immediately that
\Delta_{A}[t]
is natural. Tenseness
follows by comparing the following diagrams that we get for
any complemented subobject
\Psi\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
.

\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Psi)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}t{]}(\Psi)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}t{]}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle(1)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle(2)}

\textstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}G{]}(\Psi)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}t{]}(\Psi)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Psi)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\scriptstyle{t(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle(3)}
\textstyle{\ G(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle(4)}

The pasted rectangles are equal, and (2), (3) and (4) are
pullbacks, so (1) is too.
∎

Corollary 3.1
.

\Delta_{A}[F]
is a complemented subobject of the
shifted
F

\Delta_{A}[F]\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F\circ S_{A}

F+\Delta_{A}[F]\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.86868pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.49368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.98737pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F\circ S_{A}

where the first component is
F
of the unit

\eta_{A}\colon\id\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>S_{A}
.

3.2
Limit and colimit rules

\Delta_{A}
satisfies all the same commutation
properties with respect to limits and colimits as the

\Delta
of
Paré, (2024)
. This may be proved directly
with virtually the same proofs as in
loc. cit.

However, just as the usual properties of partial
derivatives follow from their single variable versions
by fixing all the variables but one, those of
\Delta_{A}

follow from their
\Delta
counterparts.

Proposition 3.2
.

Objects
A
in
{\bf A}
and
\Phi
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

give an affine functor
{\bf Set}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

A\!f\!f_{A,\Phi}(X)={\bf A}(A,-)\cdot X+\Phi\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

For any tense functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

and object
B
in
{\bf B}
, the translated functor

F^{B}_{A,\Phi}=({\bf Set}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47136pt\raise 6.41943pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44167pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{A\!f\!f_{A,\Phi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.23888pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.47775pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47023pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{F}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.5442pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.08841pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49382pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e\!v_{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.89236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.78473pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set})

is taut and

\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)(B)\cong\Delta[F^{B}_{A,\Phi}](0)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

The evaluation functors are tense as is
A\!f\!f_{A,\Phi}

so the composite
e\!v_{B}\circ F\circ A\!f\!f_{A,\Phi}
is
too, so taut.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Delta[F^{B}_{A,\Phi}](0)&=&F^{B}_{A,\Phi}(1)\setminus F^{B}_{A,\Phi}(0)\\
&=&F({\bf A}(A,-)\cdot 1+\Phi)(B)\setminus F({\bf A}(A,-)\cdot 0+\Phi)(B)\\
&\cong&F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\setminus F(\Phi)(B)\\
&=&\Delta_{A}[F](B)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}\end{array}

∎

Precomposing by any functor, in particular
A\!f\!f_{A,\Phi}
, preserves
all limits and colimits (of the
F
’s), and precomposing by a
functor that preserves complemented subobjects preserves tense
transformations. The same holds for postcomposing by
ev_{B}
.
Furthermore, the
ev_{B}
jointly create limits and colimits. These
considerations give the following results.

Theorem 3.1
.

(1)

If
{\bf I}
is confluent and
\Gamma\colon{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})

a diagram of tense functors (and tense transformations),
then

\Delta_{A}[\varinjlim_{I}\Gamma(I)]\cong\varinjlim_{I}\Delta_{A}[\Gamma(I)]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

(2)

If
{\bf I}
is non-empty and connected and

\Gamma\colon{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})
, then

\Delta_{A}[\varprojlim_{I}\Gamma(I)]\cong\varprojlim_{I}\Delta_{A}[\Gamma(I)]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

(3)

For any set
I
and tense functors
F_{i}

(
i\in I
) we have

\Delta_{A}\Bigl[\prod_{i\in I}F_{i}\Bigr]\cong\sum_{J\subsetneqq I}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in J}F_{j}\Bigr)\times\Bigl(\prod_{k\notin J}\Delta_{A}[F_{k}]\Bigr)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Corollary 3.2
.

(1)

\Delta_{A}[F+G]\cong\Delta_{A}[F]+\Delta_{A}[G]

(2)

\Delta_{A}[C\cdot F]\cong C\Delta_{A}[F]

(
C
a constant set)

(3)

\Delta_{A}[F\times G]\cong(\Delta_{A}[F]\times G)+(F\times\Delta_{A}[G])+(\Delta_{A}[F]\times\Delta_{A}[G])
.

We now look at a few special cases.

Proposition 3.3
.

A profunctor
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
gives
a tense
P\otimes(\ \ )\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

and
\Delta_{A}[P\otimes(\ \ )]\cong P(A,-)
.

Proof.

P\otimes(\ \ )
is cocontinuous so preserves binary coproducts

\begin{array}[]{lll}P\otimes(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))&\cong&P\otimes\Phi+P\otimes{\bf A}(A,-)\\
&\cong&P\otimes\Phi+P(A,-)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}\end{array}

∎

Corollary 3.3
.

\Delta_{A}[\id_{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}]={\bf A}(A,-)
.

All that was used in
3.3
was that

P\otimes(\ \ )
preserved binary coproducts, so we can
improve it.

Proposition 3.4
.

If
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
preserves
binary coproducts, then

\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)=F({\bf A}(A,-))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Note that
\Delta_{A}[F]
is independent of
\Phi
,
so
\Delta_{A}[F]
is the constant functor

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
with value

F({\bf A}(A,-))
.

We can do better than (2) in the corollary
3.2
.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
be
tense and
P\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
a
profunctor. Then

\Delta_{A}[P\otimes F]\cong P\otimes\Delta_{A}[F]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

We have a coproduct diagram preserved by
P\otimes(\ \ )

\textstyle{\quad\quad F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-1))\ }
\textstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\quad\quad}
\textstyle{F(\Phi)\quad}
\textstyle{\quad\quad F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-1))\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi)\quad}
\textstyle{\Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\quad\quad}
\textstyle{\longmapsto}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad P\otimes F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-1))\ }
\textstyle{P\otimes(\Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\quad\quad\quad}
\textstyle{P\otimes F(\Phi)\quad\quad\quad}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad P\otimes F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-1))\ }
\textstyle{P\otimes F(\Phi)\quad\quad\quad}
\textstyle{P\otimes(\Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\quad\quad\quad}

from which the result follows. ∎

The notation
P\otimes F
may need some explanation as
it doesn’t type check. It is componentwise tensor,

(P\otimes F)(\Phi)=P\otimes_{\bf B}F(\Phi)
.
We can interpret
3.5
as saying that
multiplying
F
by a matrix of constants is preserved by
differences. But we can generalize this result to the
following, although the interpretation of “pulling constants out”
may be lost.

Proposition 3.6
.

If
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
is tense
and
G\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf C}

preserves binary coproducts, then

\Delta_{A}[GF]=G\Delta_{A}[F]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

3.3
Analytic functors

In this section we prove that the generalized analytic
functors of
Fiore et al., (2008)
are closed under
taking differences and, in fact,
derive an explicit formula for the symmetric sequences
so obtained.

We start with an addition formula for analytic functors
which may look obvious but is frustratingly hard to
make precise. The integral notation for coends
conveniently hides the functoriality of the arguments,
which in the case at hand is not trivial, involving
permutations as it does.

We introduce some notation, without which we run the
risk of drowning in a sea of subscripts, subsubscripts,
ellipses, and so on.

In what follows
\vec{A}
represents an arbitrary object
of
!{\bf A}
,
\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle
of length

n
. Recall that a morphism
(\sigma,\langle f_{1},\dots,f_{n}\rangle)\colon\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\langle A^{\prime}_{1},\dots,A^{\prime}_{n}\rangle
is a permutation

\sigma\in S_{n}
and a sequence of morphisms

f_{i}\colon A_{\sigma i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}_{i}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

We will denote that by
(\sigma,\vec{f}\,)\colon\vec{A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{A}^{\prime}
.
We also use objects
\vec{X}=\langle X_{1},\dots,X_{k}\rangle

and
\vec{Y}=\langle Y_{1},\dots,Y_{l}\rangle
whose
lengths are
k
and
l
respectively. By construction,

!{\bf A}
is a monoidal category whose tensor is
concatenation

\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y}=\langle X_{1},\dots,X_{k}\,,\,Y_{1},\dots,Y_{l}\rangle

a notation which we use extensively. Of course, it also applies
to morphisms

(\tau,\vec{g}\,)\otimes(g,\vec{h}\,)=(\tau+\rho,\vec{g}\otimes\vec{h}\,)

where
\tau+\rho\colon k+l\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>k+l
is the ordinal sum, and

\vec{g}\otimes\vec{h}
is concatenation.

We also use the notation, and obvious variants,

\prod\Phi\vec{A}\colon=\Phi A_{1}\times\dots\times\Phi A_{n}

for
\Phi
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
. An element

\langle a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\rangle
of
\prod\Phi\vec{A}

is denoted
\vec{a}\in\prod\Phi\vec{A}
.

The addition formula alluded to above is given in the
following statement.

Theorem 3.2
.

Let
P\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
be an

{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
symmetric sequence and

\widetilde{P}\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

the analytic functor it defines. Then for
\Phi_{1}
and

\Phi_{2}
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
and
B
in
{\bf B}

we have a natural isomorphism

\widetilde{P}(\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2})(B)\cong\int^{\vec{X}}\int^{\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}\times\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The idea of the proof is simple:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}(\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2})(B)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{A}}P(\vec{A};B)\times\prod(\Phi_{1}\vec{A}+\Phi_{2}\vec{A}\,)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{A}}P(\vec{A};B)\times\sum_{\alpha\colon n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 1.99306pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2}\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{X},\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}\times\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{X}}\int^{\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}\times\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The first line is just the definition of
\widetilde{P}
, the
second line is distributivity of
\prod
over
+
, and the
last line is Fubini for coends. It’s in going from the second to
the third line that everything happens. The “reason” for the
isomorphism is that for each summand with
\Phi_{1}
and

\Phi_{2}
interspersed “at random” in the product, there is
an isomorphism in
!{\bf A}
which permutes them so that
all the
\Phi_{1}
come first followed by the
\Phi_{2}
. And,
indeed that’s the reason. The devil is in the details, as they
say.

We step back and consider how we might show that two
coends are isomorphic. Let
\Gamma\colon{\bf I}^{op}\times{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
be a functor which we might think of as a
profunctor
\Gamma\colon{\bf I}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf I}
. The
coend
\int^{I}\Gamma(I,I)
consists of equivalence
classes of elements of
\Gamma
,
[I\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.73264pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{x}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces I]
,
the equivalence relation generated by identifying

x\colon I\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}I
with
x^{\prime}\colon I^{\prime}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}I^{\prime}

when there are
f\colon I\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>I^{\prime}
and
{\bar{x}}\colon I^{\prime}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}I
such that
x={\bar{x}}f
and
x^{\prime}=f{\bar{x}}
:

\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{x^{\prime}}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}}
\textstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{{\bar{x}}}

So
x
is equivalent to
x^{\prime}
if there’s a zigzag of such
diagrams joining them.

But in the case at hand the equivalence relation is simpler
because both of the diagrams whose coends we’re
considering are separable into a product of a contravariant
functor times a covariant one.

Definition 3.3
.

A diagram
\Gamma\colon{\bf I}^{op}\times{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
is
separable
if for every
f\colon I\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>I^{\prime}
,

\textstyle{\Gamma(I^{\prime},I^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I,I^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(f,I^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I^{\prime},I)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I^{\prime},I^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(I^{\prime},f)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I^{\prime},I)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I,I)}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(f,I)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I,I)}
\textstyle{\Gamma(I,I^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma(I,f)}

is a pullback.

For example, if
\Gamma(I,I^{\prime})=\Gamma_{0}I\times\Gamma_{1}I^{\prime}

for
\Gamma_{0}\colon{\bf I}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
and

\Gamma_{1}\colon{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
, then
\Gamma
is
separable. Or, if

is a groupoid, every
\Gamma
is
separable.

The point of this definition is that the equivalence relation is
generated by identifying
x
with
x^{\prime}
when there is an

f\colon I\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>I^{\prime}
such that

:

\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}}
\textstyle{I^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{x^{\prime}}

The
{\bar{x}}
is automatic. This is important because we
can compose such squares.

Let us call an

a
\Gamma
-
algebra

and an
