as above a
homomorphism
. Then we get a
category
{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)
and
\int^{I}\Gamma(I,I)=\pi_{0}{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)
, the set of connected components
of
{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)
.

Let
\Theta\colon{\bf J}^{op}\times{\bf J}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}

be another bivariant diagram. A morphism
(\Xi,\xi)\colon\Gamma\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Theta
is a functor
\Xi\colon{\bf I}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf J}

and a natural transformation
\xi\colon\Gamma\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Theta(\Xi(-),\Xi(-))

\textstyle{{\bf I}^{op}\times{\bf I}}
\textstyle{\bf Set}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{{\bf I}^{op}\times{\bf I}}
\textstyle{{\bf J}^{op}\times{\bf J}}
\scriptstyle{\Xi^{op}\times\Xi}
\textstyle{{\bf J}^{op}\times{\bf J}}
\textstyle{\bf Set}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{\bf I}
\textstyle{\bf J}
\scriptstyle{\Xi}
\textstyle{\bf I}
\textstyle{{\bf J}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\Xi}
\textstyle{\bf I}
\textstyle{\bf I}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{\bf I}
\textstyle{\bf J}
\scriptstyle{\Xi}
\textstyle{\bf J}
\textstyle{{\bf J}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\xi}

Such a morphism induces a functor

{\bf Alg}(\Xi,\xi)\colon{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Alg}(\Theta)

\textstyle{I}
\textstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{x}
\textstyle{\Xi(I)}
\textstyle{\Xi(I)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\xi(I,I)(x)}
\textstyle{\longmapsto}

We are now ready to apply this to our addition formula. Let

\Gamma\colon!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}

be given by

\Gamma(\vec{X},\vec{Y};\vec{X}^{\prime},\vec{Y}^{\prime}\,)=P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}^{\prime}\times\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}^{\prime}

and
\Theta\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}!{\bf A}
by

\Theta(\vec{A};\vec{A}^{\prime}\,)=P(\vec{A};B)\times\sum_{\alpha\colon n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 1.99306pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2}\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}^{\prime}

with the obvious action on morphisms. Note that
\Gamma

and
\Theta
are both products of a covariant part
(with the primes) and a contravariant part (without primes)
so that they are separable. Thus we will be able to compute
the coends by taking connected components of their
categories of elements.

Theorem 3.3
.

With the above notation, there is a morphism

\textstyle{!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{\otimes}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}\times!{\bf A}}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{\otimes}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}}
\textstyle{!{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{\xi}

such that the induced functor

{\bf Alg}(\otimes,\xi)\colon{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Alg}(\Theta)

is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

Throughout,
B
is a fixed object of
{\bf B}
.

An element of
\Gamma(\vec{X},\vec{Y};\vec{X}^{\prime},\vec{Y}^{\prime}\,)

is a triple

\large(p\in P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B),\ \vec{x}\in\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}^{\prime},\ \vec{y}\in\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}^{\prime}\large)\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}

and an element of
\Theta(\vec{A},\vec{A}^{\prime}\,)
is a triple

(p\in P(\vec{A};B),\ \alpha\colon n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2,\ \vec{a}\in\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}^{\prime})\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}

where
\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}^{\prime}
is

\prod^{n^{\prime}}_{i=1}\Phi_{\alpha i}A^{\prime}_{i}
,
as expected.

\xi\colon\Gamma(\vec{X},\vec{Y};\vec{X}^{\prime},\vec{Y}^{\prime})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Theta(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y},\vec{X}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{Y}^{\prime})

is given by

\xi(p,\vec{x},\vec{y})=(p\in P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B),\ \alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\colon k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2,\ \langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\rangle\in\prod\Phi_{\alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}}(\vec{X}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{Y}^{\prime}))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Here
\alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}
is the indexing that consists of

1
’s followed by
2
’s,

\alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}(i)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{if\quad}l\leq i\leq k^{\prime}\\
2&\mbox{if\quad}k^{\prime}<i\leq k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}\end{array}\right.

and
\langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\rangle
is concatenation

\langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\rangle=\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{k^{\prime}},y_{1},\dots,y_{l^{\prime}}\rangle\in\Phi_{1}X^{\prime}_{1}\times\dots\times\Phi_{1}X^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\times\Phi_{2}Y^{\prime}_{1}\times\dots\times\Phi_{2}Y^{\prime}_{l^{\prime}}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Naturality of
\xi
is a straightforward calculation.

The morphism
(\otimes,\xi)
induces a functor

\Xi\colon{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Alg}(\Theta)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Explicitly, a
\Gamma
-algebra is a
5
-tuple

(\vec{X},\ \vec{Y},\ p\in P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B),\ \vec{x}\in\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X},\ \vec{y}\in\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y})

and a
\Theta
-algebra is a quadruple

(\vec{A},\ p\in P(\vec{A};B),\ \alpha\colon n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2,\ \vec{a}\in\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}\,)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

\Xi
assigns to
(\vec{X},\vec{Y},p,\vec{x},\vec{y}\,)
the
algebra
(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y},\ p,\ \alpha_{k,l},\ \langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\rangle)
.

A homomorphism
(\vec{X},\vec{Y},p,\vec{x},\vec{y}\,)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\vec{X}^{\prime},\vec{Y}^{\prime},p^{\prime},\vec{x}^{\prime},\vec{y}\,^{\prime})
is a pair of
morphisms in
!{\bf A}
,

(\tau,\vec{g})\colon\vec{X}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{X}^{\prime}\mbox{\quad and \quad}(\rho,\vec{h}\,)\colon\vec{Y}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{Y}^{\prime}

preserving everything. It is sent to
(\tau,\vec{g})\otimes(\rho,\vec{h}\,)
by
\Xi
.

\otimes
is faithful as it is just concatenation, so
\Xi

is also faithful.

If
(\sigma,\vec{f}\,)
is a homomorphism

(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y},\ p,\ \alpha_{k,l},\ \langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\,\rangle)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\vec{X}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{Y}^{\prime},\ p^{\prime},\ \alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}},\ \langle\vec{x}^{\prime},\vec{y}\,^{\prime}\rangle)

we have

\textstyle{2}
\textstyle{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{k+l}
\textstyle{2}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{k,l}}
\textstyle{k+l}
\textstyle{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}

which implies that
\sigma
restricts to bijections

\tau\colon k^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>k
and
\rho\colon l^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>l
(by taking
inverse images of
\{1\}
and
\{2\}
) so
k^{\prime}=k

and
l^{\prime}=l
and
\sigma=\tau+\rho
. It follows that

\vec{f}
consists of morphisms
(\tau,\vec{g})\colon\vec{X}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{X}^{\prime}
and
(\rho,\vec{h})\colon\vec{Y}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{Y}^{\prime}
and the preservation of
\langle\vec{x},\vec{y}\,\rangle
becomes preservation of
\vec{x}

and
\vec{y}\,
separately. I.e.
(\sigma,\vec{f}\,)

is
\Xi((\tau,\vec{g}),(\rho,\vec{h}))
and so

\Xi
is full.

For any
\Theta
-algebra
(\vec{A},p,\alpha,\vec{a})
,
there is a permutation of
\sigma\in S_{n}
such that

n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47765pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.79462pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.58923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49507pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.09445pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.1889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2

is order-preserving, i.e. all the
1
’s come first
and then the
2
’s, so that
\alpha\sigma=\alpha_{k,l}

where
k
is the cardinality of
\alpha^{-1}\{1\}

and
l
that of
\alpha^{-1}\{2\}
. Associated to
\sigma

is an isomorphism

(\sigma,\vec{1}\,)\colon\vec{A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{A_{\sigma}}

where
\vec{A_{\sigma}}
is
\langle A_{\sigma 1},\dots,A_{\sigma n}\rangle
and
\vec{1}=\langle 1_{A_{\sigma 1}},\dots,1_{A_{\sigma n}}\rangle
.
We can transport the
\Theta
-algebra structure on

\vec{A}
to one on
\vec{A_{\sigma}}
giving an
algebra isomorphism

(\sigma,\vec{1}\,)\colon(\vec{A},p,\alpha,\vec{a})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\vec{A_{\sigma}},\ p\cdot(\sigma^{-1},\vec{1}\,),\ \alpha_{k,l},\ \vec{a}_{\sigma})

where
p\cdot(\sigma^{-1},\vec{1}\,)=P((\sigma^{-1},\vec{1}\,);B)(p)
and

\vec{a}_{\sigma}=\langle a_{\sigma 1},\dots,a_{\sigma n}\rangle
in
\prod\Phi_{\alpha\sigma}\vec{A_{\sigma}}
. The
\Theta
-algebras
with indexing of the form
\alpha_{k,l}
are
precisely those in the image of
\Xi
. Indeed,
the
\vec{X}
are the first
k

A
’s,

\langle A_{\sigma 1},\dots,A_{\sigma k}\rangle

in this case and
\vec{Y}
the last
l
of them

\langle A_{\sigma(k+l)},\dots,A_{\sigma(n)}\rangle
.
Similarly
\vec{x}=\langle a_{\sigma 1},\dots,a_{\sigma k}\rangle
and
\vec{y}=\langle a_{\sigma(k+1)},\dots a_{\sigma n}\rangle
.
Then
\Xi(\vec{X},\vec{Y},p\cdot(\sigma^{-1},\vec{1}\,),\vec{x},\vec{y}\,)
is
(\vec{A_{\sigma}},p\cdot(\sigma^{-1},\vec{1}\,),\alpha_{k,l},\vec{a})
, so
\Xi
is essentially
surjective, which shows it’s an equivalence.
∎

If we take connected components we get

\pi_{0}{\bf Alg}(\Gamma)\cong\pi_{0}{\bf Alg}(\Theta)

so the coend of
\Gamma
is isomorphic to that of

\Theta
.

Corollary 3.4
.

\int^{\vec{X},\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi_{1}\vec{X}\times\prod\Phi_{2}\vec{Y}\cong\int^{\vec{A}}P(\vec{A};B)\times\sum_{\alpha\colon n\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.11113pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.49808pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.99615pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>2}\prod\Phi_{\alpha}\vec{A}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Our addition formula, Theorem
3.2
,
now follows by a simple application of the Fubini theorem
for coends, which is what we wanted, but
Theorem
3.3
is a stronger result.

Our next step in the derivation of the formula for

\Delta_{A}[\widetilde{P}]
is to specialize our addition
formula to the case
\Phi_{1}=\Phi
and

\Phi_{2}={\bf A}(A,-)
. This gives

\widetilde{P}(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)=\int^{\vec{X}}\int^{\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi\vec{X}\times\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})

in which the expression

\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})={\bf A}(A,Y_{1})\times\dots\times{\bf A}(A,Y_{l})

appears, not surprisingly, as it already appears
in the definition of
\widetilde{P}
. It defines a
functor

\prod{\bf A}(A,-)\colon!{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}

closely related to the representable functor

!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},-)
where
A^{\otimes n}=\langle A,\dots,A\rangle
, the
n
-fold
tensor of
A
.

Proposition 3.7
.

With the above notation we have

\prod{\bf A}(A,-)\cong\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},-)/S_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

If
\vec{Y}=\langle Y_{1},\dots,Y_{l}\rangle
, then

!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})
is
0
unless

l=n
in which case an element of
!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})
is a morphism

(\sigma,\vec{f}\,)\colon A^{\otimes n}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\vec{Y}

so that
!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})\cong S_{n}\times{\bf A}(A_{1}Y_{1})\times\dots\times{\bf A}(A,Y_{n})
and if we quotient by

S_{n}
we get

!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})/{S_{n}}\cong\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})

easily seen to be natural in
\vec{Y}
. The result
follows.
∎

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
W\colon!{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
. Then

\int^{\vec{Y}}W(\vec{Y})\times\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})\cong\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}W(A^{\otimes n})/S_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{Y}}W({\vec{Y}})\times\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{\vec{Y}}W(\vec{Y})\times\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})/S_{n}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}\int^{\vec{Y}}W(\vec{Y})\times(!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})/{S_{n}}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}\Bigl(\int^{\vec{Y}}W(\vec{Y})\times!{\bf A}(A^{\otimes n},\vec{Y})\Bigr)/S_{n}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}W(A^{\otimes n})/S_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The second isomorphism is commutation of coends and
coproducts, the third commutation of coends with colimits
(“modding out” by
S_{n}

is
a colimit), the last
isomorphism comes from the fact that tensoring with a representable
is substitution.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

\widetilde{P}(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\cong\int^{\vec{X}}\sum^{\infty}_{n=0}P(\vec{X}\otimes A^{\otimes n};B)/(\{\id_{k}\}\times S_{n})\times\prod\Phi\vec{X}

Proof.

\widetilde{P}(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\cong\int^{\vec{X}}\int^{\vec{Y}}P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi\vec{X}\times\prod{\bf A}(A,\vec{Y})\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

If we fix
\vec{X}
and consider the coend over
\vec{Y}
,
we can apply the previous lemma with

W(\vec{Y})=P(\vec{X}\otimes\vec{Y};B)\times\prod\Phi\vec{X}

and the result follows immediately.
∎

Corollary 3.6
.

\Delta_{A}[\widetilde{P}](\Phi)(B)\cong\int^{\vec{X}}\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}P(\vec{X}\otimes A^{\otimes n};B)/(\{\id_{k}\}\times S_{n})\times\prod\Phi\vec{X}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

The inclusion
\widetilde{P}(\Phi)\hookrightarrow\widetilde{P}(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))
corresponds to the
n=0
summand.
∎

For any
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
symmetric sequence

P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
and object
A
of

{\bf A}
we define a new symmetric sequence

\nabla_{A}P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
by the formula

\nabla_{A}P(\vec{X};B)=\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}P(\vec{X}\otimes A^{\otimes n};B)/(\{\id_{k}\}\times S_{n})\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Now Corollary
3.6
can be stated in its final
form, giving the main theorem of the section.

Theorem 3.4
.

Analytic functors
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

are closed under taking differences. If
P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
is a symmetric sequence, then

\Delta_{A}[\widetilde{P}]\cong\widetilde{\nabla_{A}P}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The definition of
\nabla_{A}P
as a coproduct of quotients
is clear but for formal manipulations a more abstract
definition is useful. Let
{\bf S}_{+}
be the category
whose objects are positive finite cardinals,
k>0
,
and whose morphisms are bijections. So
{\bf S}_{+}

is the coproduct

{\bf S}_{+}=\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}{\bf S}_{k}

where
{\bf S}_{k}
is the symmetric group
{\bf S}_{k}

considered as a one-object category.

Given an
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
symmetric sequence

P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
and an object

A
of
{\bf A}
we get an
{\bf S}_{+}
family of

{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
symmetric sequences

P_{A}\colon{\bf S}^{op}_{+}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}(!{\bf A},{\bf B})

P_{A}(k)(A_{1}\dots A_{n};B)=P(A_{1}\dots A_{n},A,A,\dots,A;B)

where there are
k

A
’s. Functoriality and naturality
are obvious. Now
\nabla_{A}P=\varinjlim_{k}P_{A}(k)
.

Proposition 3.8
.

For
P\colon!{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
an
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}

symmetric sequence and
Q\colon{\bf B}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf C}
a
profunctor, we have

\nabla_{A}(Q\otimes P)\cong Q\otimes\nabla_{A}P\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

\displaystyle\nabla_{A}(Q\otimes P)(A_{1}\dots A_{n};C)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{k}\int^{B}Q(B,C)\times P(A_{1}\dots A_{n},A\dots A;B)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{B}Q(B,C)\times\varinjlim_{k}P(A_{1}\dots A_{n},A\dots A;B)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\int^{B}Q(B,C)\times\nabla_{A}P(A_{1}\dots A_{n};B)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle(Q\otimes\nabla_{A}P)(A_{1}\dots A_{n};C)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

∎

Corollary 3.7
.

For any
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
symmetric sequence
P

we have

\nabla_{A}P\cong P\otimes\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

\nabla_{A}P\cong\nabla_{A}(P\otimes\Id_{!{\bf A}})\cong P\otimes\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

∎

\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}
is easy to describe:

\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}\colon{\bf Set}^{!{\bf A}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{!{\bf A}}

\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}(A_{1}\dots A_{n};A^{\prime}_{1}\dots A^{\prime}_{m})\cong\sum^{\infty}_{k=1}!{\bf A}(A_{1}\dots A_{n},A\dots A;A^{\prime}_{1}\dots A^{\prime}_{m})/(\{\id_{n}\}\times S_{k})

which is
0
if
m\leq n
and

!{\bf A}(A_{1}\dots A_{n},A,\dots,A;A^{\prime}_{1}\dots A^{\prime}_{m})/(\{\id_{n}\}\times S_{m-n})

when
m>n
. There are
m-n

A
’s and the
action we’re modding out by is
S_{m-n}
acting
on those
A
’s.

There is also a generic difference formula.

Corollary 3.8
.

\Delta_{A}[\widetilde{P}]\cong P\otimes\Delta_{A}[\Id_{!{\bf A}}]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Delta_{A}\widetilde{P}&\cong\widetilde{\nabla_{A}P}&(\mbox{Thm.~\ref{Thm-DeltaAnalytic}})\\
&\cong(P\otimes\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}){\widetilde{\ \ \ }}&(\mbox{Cor.~\ref{Cor-NablaLin}})\\
&\cong P\otimes\widetilde{\nabla_{A}\Id_{!{\bf A}}}&(\mbox{Cor.~\ref{Cor-TildeLin}})\\
&\cong P\otimes\Delta_{A}{[}\Id_{!{\bf A}}{]}&(\mbox{Thm.~\ref{Thm-DeltaAnalytic}})\end{array}

∎

3.4
Higher differences

As
\Delta_{A}[F]
is also tense, its difference can also
be taken
\Delta_{A^{\prime}}[\Delta_{A}[F]]=\Delta_{A^{\prime},A}[F]

and so on, iteratively. For any sequence
\langle A_{1}\dots A_{n}\rangle
of length
n
of objects of
{\bf A}
we
define

\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}{[}F{]}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}F&\mbox{\ if\ }n=0\\
\Delta_{A_{1}}{[}\Delta_{\langle A_{2}\dots A_{n}\rangle}{[}F{]}{]}&\mbox{\ if\ }n\geq 1\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}\end{array}\right.

Definition 3.4
.

We say that an element of
F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\cdots+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))(B)
is
new
(for
\langle A_{1},\cdots,A_{n}\rangle)
) if it is not in any

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+\cdots+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-))(B)
for any proper mono

\alpha\colon k\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.49997pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 8.99994pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.98901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\ n
.

If an element is in
F
of a subsum, it’s in
every bigger subsum, so it is sufficient to consider
only those subsums with one less term.Thus the
new elements are those in the set difference

F(\Phi+\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}F(\Phi+\sum_{i\neq j}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Theorem 3.5
.

The higher difference
\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}{[}F{]}(\Phi)

consists of the new elements of

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\cdots+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))
.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
n
. For

n=0,1
the result holds by definition. Assume the
result holds for sequences of length
n-1
and take

\langle A_{i}\rangle=\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle
.
Let
\langle A_{i}\rangle^{+}=\langle A_{2},\dots,A_{n}\rangle
.

An element of
\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F](\Phi)(B)
is
an element of
\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle^{+}}[F](\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-))(B)
which is not in

\Delta_{\langle A_{I}\rangle^{+}}[F](\Phi)(B)
. An element of

\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle^{+}}[F](\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-))(B)
is, by the induction hypothesis, an element of

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\sum^{n}_{i=2}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)\cong F(\Phi+\sum^{n}_{i=1}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)

(1)

not in

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+\sum^{n}_{i=2,i\neq j}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)\cong F(\Phi+\sum^{n}_{i=1,i\neq j}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)

(2)

for any
2\leq j\leq n
. From this we must exclude
the elements of
\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle^{+}}[F](\Phi)(B)

and these, again by the induction hypothesis, are
elements of

F(\Phi+\sum^{n}_{i=2}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(\Phi)(B)

(3)

except for any in some

F(\Phi+\sum^{n}_{i=2,i\neq j}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(\Phi)(B)

(4)

for
2\leq j\leq n
.

To summarize,

\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F](\Phi)(B)=((1)\setminus(2))\setminus((3)\setminus(4))\hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}

but
(4)\subseteq(2)
so

\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F](\Phi)(B)=(1)\setminus((2)\cup(3))\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Now
(2)\cup(3)
is the union of

F(\Phi+\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)

over all
j,

1\leq j\leq n
, and the result follows.
∎

We see from this formula that
\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F]

is independent of the order of the differences, a version of
Clairaut’s theorem.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
\langle A_{i}\rangle
be a sequence of length
n

of objects of
{\bf A}
and
\sigma\in S_{n}
a permutation,
then

\Delta_{\langle A_{\sigma i}\rangle}[F]\cong\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F]\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

4
The discrete Jacobian

4.1
Definitions and functoriality

Let
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
be a
tense functor and let
f\colon A\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A^{\prime}
be a morphism of

{\bf A}
. Then, as

\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\scriptstyle{\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-)}

is a pullback of complemented objects, so is

\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\scriptstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-))}

and it follows that
F(\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-))
restricts to
complements giving another pullback

\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ \ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A^{\prime}}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ \ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{f}{[}F{]}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta_{A^{\prime}}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\scriptstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-))}

This proves the following:

Proposition 4.1
.

For any
\Phi
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
,

\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)
is functorial in
A
, i.e. is the object part of a functor

\Delta[F](\Phi)\colon{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

By exponential adjointness we get a functor

{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
, i.e. a profunctor

{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
.

Definition 4.1
.

The
(discrete) Jacobian profunctor
of
F
at
\Phi

\Delta[F](\Phi)\colon{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}

is given by

\Delta[F](\Phi)(A,B)=\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

It’s more or less clear that
\Delta[F](\Phi)
is functorial
in
\Phi
, which we express in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.2
.

For any tense functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
,
\Delta[F](\Phi)
is the object
part of a tense functor

\Delta[F]\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}}={\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}({\bf A},{\bf B})\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

Proof.

For a natural transformation
t\colon\Phi\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Psi
and
object
A
in
{\bf A}
,

\textstyle{\ \Psi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Psi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Psi\ }
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\ \Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Phi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Psi+{\bf A}(A,-)\ }
\scriptstyle{t+{\bf A}(A,-)}

is a pullback of a complemented subobject, so

\textstyle{\ F\Psi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F\Psi\ }
\scriptstyle{Ft}
\textstyle{\ F\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\scriptstyle{F(t+{\bf A}(A,-))}

is too. So
F(t+{\bf A}(A,-))
restricts to the complements,
giving another pullback

\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}\Psi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}}
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}\Psi\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}t}
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}\Phi\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Psi+{\bf A}(A,-))\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,,\hss}}
\scriptstyle{F(t+{\bf A}(A,-))}

(*)

hence functoriality.

We still must prove that it is tense.

Proposition
3.1
says that for a fixed

A
,
\Delta_{A}[F]\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

is tense and
\Delta_{A}[F]
is the composite

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.46855pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta[F]}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.09116pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.18231pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.46019pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{ev_{B}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.44206pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.88412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}

The
ev_{B}
are the evaluation functors which preserve
pullbacks and collectively reflect them, so that
\Delta[F]

will preserve pullbacks of complemented subobjects.
However, the
ev_{B}
don’t reflect complemented
subobjects, so we still must show that
\Delta[F]

preserves those.

Let
\Phi_{0}\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
be a complemented
subobject. We want to show that
\Delta[F](\Phi_{0})\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta[F](\Phi)
is complemented, or
equivalently, for every
f\colon A^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A
and

g\colon B\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>B^{\prime}

\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(f,g)}
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})\ }
\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(f,g)}

is a pullback. We can do this separately for
f
and
g
,
fixing
B
and then
A
. We already know for fixed

A
it’s a pullback. So let’s fix
B
.

Let
f\colon A^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A
and consider

\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))(B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A,^{\prime}B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A^{\prime},B)\ }

\scriptstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-))(B)}

\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(f,B)}

\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(f,B)}

Pb

and

\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))(B)\ \hbox to0.0pt{\,.\hss}}
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi_{0}+{\bf A}(A,-))(B)\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi_{0}+{\bf A}(A^{\prime},-))(B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(A,B)\ }
\textstyle{\ \Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)(A^{\prime},B)\ }

\scriptstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(f,-))(B)}

\scriptstyle{F(\Phi_{0}+{\bf A}(f,-))(B)}

\scriptstyle{\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})(f,B)}

Pb
Pb

The second and third squares are pullbacks by
(*)

and the fourth because
F
is tense. As the composite
of the first and second squares is equal to the composite
of the third and fourth, we get that the first square is a
pullback, which shows that
\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi_{0})\ \ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)
is complemented.
∎

To complete the discussion of functoriality of
\Delta

note that
\Delta_{A}[F](\Phi)
is a subfunctor of

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A,-))
which is not only functorial
in
\Phi
and
A
but by Proposition
3.1

also in
F
but only for tense transformations.
Proposition
2.9
says that the evaluation
functors
ev_{B}
jointly reflect tenseness of transformations, so that

\Delta_{A}[t]
itself will be tense. Thus we get a functor

\Delta\colon{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}})

the
(discrete) Jacobian functor
.

There are various ways of reformulating the Jacobian which
are of independent interest.

Given a tense functor

F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
, we get
another tense functor analogous to the differential operator

D[F]\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

D[F](\Phi,\Psi)=\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)\otimes_{\bf A}\Psi

where
\Psi
is considered as a profunctor

{\bf 1}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}
.

Definition 4.2
.

D[F]
is called the
difference operator
.

In
(
Paré,, 2024
)
we
used the finite projection
{\bf Set}\times{\bf Set}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
as a tangent bundle and saw that this
supported a definition of functorial differences where
the lax chain-rule was actually a lax functor. This
generalizes to the multivariable setting. We define

\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{P_{1}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\times{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{T{[}F{]}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\times{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{P_{1}}

by
T[F](\Phi,\Psi)=(F\Phi,\Delta{[}F{]}(\Phi)\otimes_{\bf A}\Psi)
.
We see that
T[F]
preserves colimits in the second variable.

Definition 4.3
.

T[F]
is called the
(discrete) tangent functor
.

Profunctors
{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
are in bijection with profunctors
{\bf B}^{op}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf A}^{op}
:

\frac{P\colon{\bf A}^{op}\times{\bf B}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}}{P^{\top}\colon({\bf B}^{op})\times{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}}

i.e.
P^{\top}(B,A)=P(A,B)
, the transpose as matrices.
This gives the reverse difference operator

\Delta^{\top}[F]\colon{\bf Set}^{A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}({\bf B}^{op},{\bf A}^{op}).

Definition 4.4
.

\Delta^{\top}[F]
is the
reverse difference operator
.

This suggests that we
take as the cotangent bundle the first projection
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\times{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
. As the Yoneda
embedding
Y\colon{\bf A}^{op}\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

is the cocompletion of
{\bf A}
,
the category of cocontinuous functors

{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}

is equivalent to the category of functors

{\bf A}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}

i.e.
{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}}
. So
{\bf Set}^{{\bf A}^{op}}

has a legitimate claim to be the (linear) dual of
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
.
