we get an element

t[p,\phi]\in F(\Phi)(B)
.

On the other hand
u\colon P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>FQ
is a doubly
indexed family of functions

\langle P(A_{1},{\dots},A_{n};B)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)\rangle

natural in
\langle A_{1},{\dots},A_{n}\rangle\in\ {\downarrow}{\bf A}

and
B
in
{\bf B}
.

Given
t
we get
u
by restricting to the case

\Phi=\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
and
\phi
the identity

u(p)=t[p,\id_{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}]\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Given
u
we get
t
by

t[p,\phi]=F(\phi)(u(p))\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

There is nothing to check, such as naturality or
well-definedness, as it all follows by the general
theory of Kan extensions. We will use these formulas
in the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Another result that will be useful is the following
fact which, although trivial, is interesting in its own
right and worth pointing out.

Lemma 5.1
.

For a pair
(p\colon\langle A_{i},{\dots},A_{n}\rangle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B,\phi\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi)
,
the Boolean image of
\phi

\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\rm Bim}(\phi)\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi

is an invariant of the equivalence class
[p,\phi]
.

Proof.

Suppose
(p,\phi)
and
(q,\psi)
are related by
a single morphism
(\sigma,\langle f_{j}\rangle)
of

{\downarrow}{\bf A}
, i.e.

\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},{\dots},C_{m}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\langle A_{1},{\dots},A_{n}\rangle}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\langle A_{1},{\dots},A_{n}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},{\dots},C_{m}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma,\langle f_{j}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\scriptstyle{\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{j},-)}

commute. Because
(\sigma,\langle f_{J}\rangle)
is in

{\downarrow}{\bf A}
,
\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{j},-)

is
\pi_{0}
-surjective, so
{\rm Bim}(\phi)={\rm Bim}(\psi)
.
∎

Definition 5.2
.

A functor of the form

for

.

Proposition 5.2
.

Analytic functors are soft analytic.

Proof.

!{\bf A}
of Section

is a

, and the

of

5.3
The Newton series comonad

In this section we show that taking iterated differences
is right adjoint to summation of a multivariable symmetric
series. We first combine all the iterated differences
into one soft symmetric sequence.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}

be tense. Then taking the iterated symmetric differences
of
F
evaluated at
\Phi
gives an
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}

symmetric sequence

\Delta_{*}[F](\Phi)\colon{\downarrow}{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B

\Delta_{*}[F](\Phi)(A_{1},\dots,A_{n};B)=\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}[F](\Phi)(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Proof.

\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}[F](\Phi)(B)=\Delta_{\langle A_{i}\rangle}[F](\Phi)(B)
consists
of the new elements of

F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\ ,\hss}

i.e. those elements not
in
F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-))
for any proper subsequence

\langle A_{\alpha 1},\dots,A_{\alpha k}\rangle
,

\alpha\colon k\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern 8.0001pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n
a proper mono. We’ll
show that
\Delta_{*}[F]
is a subfunctor of

F(\Phi+Q)
. Let
(\sigma,\langle f_{1},\dots,f_{m}\rangle))\colon\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle
be a morphism in
{\downarrow}{\bf A}
, and
let

be an element of

\Delta_{C_{1}}\dots\Delta_{C_{m}}[F](\Phi)(B)\subseteq F(\Phi+{\bf A}(C_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{m},-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Then
y=F(\sigma,\langle f_{1},\dots,f_{m}\rangle)(B)(x)

is an element of
F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-)(B)
and suppose it’s
not new. There is a proper monomorphism
\alpha\colon k\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern 8.0001pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n

such that

.

The pullback of a proper mono along an epi is again proper
so we get

\textstyle{k\ }
\textstyle{n}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{l\ }
\textstyle{k\ }
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{l\ }
\textstyle{m}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{m}
\textstyle{n}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
Pb

which, in turn, gives a pullback of complemented subobjects
in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}

\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-)\ }
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(C_{\beta 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{\beta l},-)\ }
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-)\ }
\scriptstyle{(\rho,\langle f_{\beta 1},\dots,f_{\beta n}\rangle)}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(C_{\beta 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{\beta l},-)\ }
\textstyle{\ {\bf A}(C_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{m},-)}
\textstyle{\ {\bf A}(C_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{m},-)}
\textstyle{{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma,\langle f_{1},\dots,f_{m}\rangle)}
Pb

Adding
\Phi
produces another such pullback and

F
, being tense, will preserve it

\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-))\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(C_{\beta 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{\beta l},-))\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{\alpha k},-))\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(C_{\beta 1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{\beta l},-))\ }
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(C_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{m},-))}
\textstyle{\ F(\Phi+{\bf A}(C_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(C_{m},-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
Pb

Then
x
in the upper right corner gets sent to

which
is in the lower left corner, so

itself is in the upper
left corner, i.e.
x
wasn’t new after all. Thus

\Delta_{*}[F](\Phi)
is a subfunctor of
F(\Phi+Q)
.
∎

\Delta_{*}[F](\Phi)
is functorial in
F
. Indeed, applying

recursively, we see that
any tense transformation
t\colon F\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>G
restricts to

\textstyle{\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))}
\textstyle{\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}{[}G{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}{[}F{]}(\Phi)\ }
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))}
\textstyle{F(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))}
\textstyle{G(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))}
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi+{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{n},-))}

which will be natural and functorial automatically. Thus for
each

in
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}
we get a functor

\Delta[\ \ ](\Phi)\colon{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathcal{}P}\!{\it rof}({\downarrow}{\bf A},{\bf B})\hbox to0.0pt{\ ,\hss}

i.e.
\Delta_{*}[F](\Phi)
is an
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
soft
symmetric sequence.

The main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 5.1
.

\Delta_{*}[\ \ ](0)\mbox{\ is right adjoint to\ }\tilde{(\ )}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Proof.

\widetilde{P}
is the left Kan extension of
P
along

Q

\textstyle{({\downarrow}{\bf A})^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{P}
\textstyle{({\downarrow}{\bf A})^{op}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\scriptstyle{Q}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf A}}
\textstyle{{\bf Set}^{\bf B}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm Lan}_{Q}P=\widetilde{P}}

so for any functor
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
we have a bijection

\widetilde{P}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48463pt\raise 5.15279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.99506pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.99011pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F\over P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.84459pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48056pt\raise-4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.68918pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>FQ

as discussed above. Now
\Delta_{*}[F](0)
is a
subfunctor of
FQ
. Indeed

\Delta_{*}[F](0)\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle(B)=\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{n}}[F](0)(B)

consists of the new elements of

We’ll show that
t\colon\widetilde{P}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F
is tense
if and only if

factors through
\Delta_{*}[F](0)\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>FQ
which will establish the theorem.

First assume
t
is tense. Let

be in

so

is in

is in
F
of some subsum

of the indices. Tenseness of

applied to the complemented subsum

\mu\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha i},-)\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
gives a pullback

\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\int^{\langle C_{j}\rangle\in{\downarrow}{\bf A}}P(\langle C_{j}\rangle;B)\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}(\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-),\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha i},-))(B)}}

\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)}
\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha i},-))(B)\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}}

is in

and
\psi\colon\sum{\bf A}(C_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha i},-)

we see

{\rm Bim}(\mu\psi)={\rm Bim}(\id)=\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
. It follows that

is the identity,
so

is not contained in
F
of any proper
subsum, i.e. is new. This gives our factorization of
u

through
\Delta_{*}[F](0)
.

Conversely, assume that
u
factors through

\Delta_{*}[F](0)
. We’ll show that
t
is tense.
Let
\Psi\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
be a complemented subobject.
We must show that

\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\widetilde{P}(\Psi)}}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F(\Psi)}}
\scriptstyle{t(\Psi)}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\widetilde{P}(\Psi)}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}(\Phi)}
\textstyle{F(\Phi)}
\scriptstyle{t(\Phi)}
\textstyle{F(\Phi)}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F(\Psi)}}

(*)

is a pullback. Take an element
[p\colon\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B,\phi\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\Phi]

of
\widetilde{P}(\Phi)(B)
and assume
t(\Phi)[p,\phi]=F(\phi)(p)
is in
F(\Psi)
. Form the pullback

\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha j},-)}}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\Psi}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha j},-)}}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\Psi}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}
Pb

It is induced by a monomorphism
\alpha\colon m\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-4.49997pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 8.99994pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.49313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n
because a complemented subobject
of a sum of representables is a subsum. We get a new
pullback now by tenseness of
F

\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha j},-))}}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}}
\scriptstyle{F(\psi)}
\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha j},-))}}
\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))}
\textstyle{F\Phi}
\scriptstyle{F(\phi)}
\textstyle{F\Phi}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{F\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}}
Pb

F(\phi)
takes
u(p)
to an element of
F(\Psi)
so

u(p)\in F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{\alpha j},-))
. But
u(p)

was supposed to be a new element of
F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))
so
\alpha
is not a proper subsum which means
that

\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\mbox{\rule[-7.11317pt]{0.0pt}{19.91692pt}}{\Psi\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}}}

Thus
[p,\phi]
is in
\widetilde{P}(\Psi)
. This shows that
our square
(*)
is indeed a pullback.
∎

The adjoint pair
\tilde{(\ )}\dashv\Delta_{*}[\ \ ](0)
induces
a comonad on
{\mathcal{}T}\!\!{\it ense}({\bf Set}^{\bf A},{\bf Set}^{\bf B})

which we call the
Newton series comonad
.

5.4
Convergence

In this section we show that the Newton series for a soft
analytic functor “converges to it”.

Theorem 5.2
.

For every
{\bf A}
-
{\bf B}
soft symmetric sequence

P\colon{\downarrow}{\bf A}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}{\bf B}
,
the unit for the adjunction of
5.1

P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta_{*}[\widetilde{P}](0)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

An element of
\Delta_{*}[\widetilde{P}](0)
at

\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle,B
is a new element
of
\widetilde{P}(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)
, i.e. of

\int^{C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\in{\downarrow}{\bf A}}P(C_{1},\dots,C_{m};B)\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}(\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-),\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))

which is an equivalence class

\big[p\colon\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B,\phi\colon\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\big]

(satisfying the newness condition, of course).

The unit
P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta_{*}[\widetilde{P}](0)
takes

to the equivalence class

\big[p\colon\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.78978pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.5pt\raise-1.62846pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip-2.84526pt\bullet\hskip-2.84526pt}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.98627pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}}B,\id\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\big]\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

A
\phi

, of the form

and we can take
its Boolean factorization by factoring
\alpha

(in
{\bf Set}

\textstyle{m}
\textstyle{k\ }
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{m}
\textstyle{n}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{n}
\textstyle{k\ }
\scriptstyle{\mu}

is a morphism of

and we have

\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma,\langle f_{j}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\scriptstyle{\id}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}

\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\scriptstyle{\sum_{\alpha}{\bf A}(f_{j},-)}

so

, which shows that the unit

P\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Delta_{*}[\widetilde{P}](0)

p\longmapsto[p,\id]

is onto.

To show that the unit is one-one we must show that if

[p,\id]=[q,\id]
then
p=q
.
[p,\id]=[q,\id]
means
there’s a zigzag path of

\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{{\bar{q}}}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{{\bar{p}}}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\rho,\langle h_{j}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(D_{s},-)}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\textstyle{\quad\quad\quad\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(D_{s},-)}
\scriptstyle{\sum_{\rho}{\bf A}(h_{j},-)}

with

in
{\downarrow}{\bf A}

joining

to
[q,\id]
. The Boolean image of

\phi_{i}
(and
\psi_{i}
) is an invariant of the equivalence

) and as

, all the

and

are actually morphisms in
{\downarrow}{\bf A}
,
i.e.
\sigma\colon m\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n
and
\tau\colon r\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n

are surjections. Now we have

\textstyle{\langle A_{i},\dots,A_{n}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\tau,\langle g_{s}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle A_{i},\dots,A_{n}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma,\langle f_{j}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\rho,\langle h_{j}\rangle)}
\textstyle{B}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{{\bar{q}}}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{{\bar{p}}}
\textstyle{\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle}
\textstyle{\langle D_{1},\dots,D_{r}\rangle}

commuting
{\bar{p}}(\sigma,\langle f_{j}\rangle)={\bar{q}}(\tau,\langle g_{s}\rangle)
at every stage of the path
joining
(p,\id)
to
(q,\id)
, and for these endpoints
we get
p
and
q
respectively, i.e.
p=q
.
∎

This shows that the Newton series comonad is idempotent.

Corollary 5.2
.

If
F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
is soft
analytic (in particular analytic) then its Newton series
converges to it, i.e. the counit

\widetilde{\Delta_{*}[F](0)}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>F

is an isomorphism.

5.5
Concluding remark

In the previous sections, we touted the functor taking

F
to
{\bar{F}}=\widetilde{\Delta_{*}[F](0)}
as a
categorical version of the Newton summation formulas
at the beginning of
5.1
, but in fact it
looks nothing like them.

Let’s consider the first one

{\bar{f}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\sum_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n}}\frac{\Delta^{k_{1}}_{x_{1}}{\dots}\Delta^{k_{n}}_{x_{n}}[f](0,\dots,0)}{k_{1!}\dots k_{n!}}\ x^{\downarrow k_{1}}_{1}\dots x^{\downarrow k_{n}}_{n}

where
f
is a function
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{R}}

and the sum is taken over all
n
-tuples of natural numbers.
We’ve replaced
f
by a (tense) functor
{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}
and the difference operators by our
functorial ones, but it’s not clear how to interpret the rest
of the formula. Let’s look at it more carefully.

The first thing to note is that, while the
x_{i}
in

\Delta_{x_{i}}
and in
x_{i}^{\downarrow k_{i}}
refer to the
same thing, they play different roles. The
x_{i}
in

\Delta_{x_{i}}
is merely a subscript indicating which
difference operator is used, and we could well have written

\Delta_{i}
instead, although
\Delta_{x_{i}}
is more
descriptive. The
x_{i}
in
x_{i}^{\downarrow k_{i}}
, on
the other hand, represents a variable which can take values,

c_{i}
. So we have

{\bar{f}}(c_{1},\dots,c_{n})=\sum_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n}}\frac{\Delta^{k_{1}}_{x_{1}}{\dots}\Delta^{k_{n}}_{x_{n}}[f](0,\dots,0)}{k_{1!}\dots k_{n!}}\ c^{\downarrow k_{1}}_{1}\dots c^{\downarrow k_{n}}_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Here all the like
\Delta
’s have been grouped together
which is fine as we have finitely many variables and they’re
totally ordered. It would be more natural to sum over all
finite sequences of variables
\langle x_{\alpha(1)}\dots x_{\alpha(m)}\rangle
and group the terms together by the
length
m
. Of course we get more terms:

\Delta^{k_{1}}_{x_{1}}\dots\Delta^{k_{n}}_{x_{n}}
gets counted

\binom{k_{1}+{\dots}+k_{n}}{k_{1},\dots,k_{n}}\ =\ \frac{(k_{1}+{\dots}+k_{n})!}{k_{1}!\dots k_{n}!}\ =\ \frac{m!}{k_{1}!\dots k_{n}!}

times, so now we have

{\bar{f}}(c_{1},\dots,c_{n})=\sum_{\alpha\colon m\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 1.99306pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.99725pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.99725pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.9945pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>n}\frac{\Delta_{\alpha(1)}\dots\Delta_{\alpha(m)}[f](0,0)}{m!}\ c^{\downarrow k_{1}}_{1}\dots c^{\downarrow k_{n}}_{n}\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

In fact this takes care of the finiteness and total ordering
of the variables, as far as the
\Delta
part of the formula
is concerned. We take a set of variables
{\rm Var}
and
consider the free monoid on it
{\rm Var}^{*}
, over which
the sum is to be taken. The
c_{i}
are a choice of value
for each variable
\phi\colon{\rm Var}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{R}}

but we still have to deal with the
k_{i}
in this setup.

The
k
’s count the number of occurrences of a given
variable
y
in a sequence
\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\rangle
.
Let

\delta\colon{\rm Var}\times{\rm Var}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{N}}

be the Kronecker delta, i.e.
\delta(x,y)=1
if
x=y

and
0
otherwise. For each
y
, extend
\delta(-,y)

to a function
\delta(-,y)\colon{\rm Var}^{*}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{N}}

using the additive structure of
{\mathbb{N}}
, so

\delta(x_{1},\dots,x_{n};y)=\sum^{m}_{i=1}\delta(x_{i},y)

is exactly the number of
y
’s in
\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\rangle
.
Thus we end up with the Newton series in the form we want

{\bar{f}}(\phi)=\sum_{\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\rangle\in{\rm Var}^{*}}\frac{\Delta_{x_{1}}\dots\Delta_{x_{m}}[f](0)}{m!}\prod_{y\in{\rm Var}}\phi(y)^{\downarrow\delta(x_{1},\dots x_{n};y)}

which, admittedly, looks more complicated than the original but
it’s the closest we can get to the categorical version.

Now the Newton series comonad of Section
5.3

\widetilde{\Delta_{*}[F](0)}=\int^{\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{m}\rangle\in\downarrow{\bf A}}\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A_{m}}[F](0)\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}({\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{m},-),\Phi)

looks similar to the above, with the following
correspondences:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}f\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{R}}&\leftrightarrow&F\colon{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}^{\bf B}\\
\mbox{variables }x&\leftrightarrow&\mbox{objects }A\mbox{ of }{\bf A}\\
{\rm Var}&\leftrightarrow&{\bf A}\\
{\rm Var}^{*}&\leftrightarrow&{\downarrow}{\bf A}\\
\phi\colon{\rm Var}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\mathbb{R}}&\leftrightarrow&\Phi\colon{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}\\
\delta(x,y)&\leftrightarrow&{\bf A}(A^{\prime},A)\\
\delta(x_{1},\dots,x_{n},y)&\leftrightarrow&{\bf A}(A_{1},A)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{m},A)\\
\prod{\phi(y)}^{\downarrow\delta(x_{1}\dots x_{n};y)}&\leftrightarrow&{\bf Set}^{\bf A}({\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{m},-),\Phi)\end{array}

The correspondence is not perfect, of course.
{\rm Var}^{*}
might
rightly be said to correspond to
!{\bf A}
rather than

{\downarrow}{\bf A}
. Then the
m!
in the sum is incroporated
in the coend via the symmetric groups.

Also
\prod\phi(y)^{\downarrow\delta(x_{1},\dots,x_{m},y)}

should correspond to monomorphisms

{\bf A}(A_{1},-)+{\dots}+{\bf A}(A_{m},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi

rather than arbitrary natural
transformations. That’s what the extra morphisms in

{\downarrow}{\bf A}
(involving surjections
\sigma
)
take care of. We need a bit more theory to explain this.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
\Phi\colon{\bf A}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>{\bf Set}
and
x\in\Phi A
.
An
ancestor
of
x
is a
y\in\Phi A^{\prime}
for which
there is a morphism
f\colon A^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A
such that

\Phi(f)(y)=x
. Two elements
x_{1}\in\Phi A_{1}
and

x_{2}\in\Phi A_{2}
are
relatives
if they have
a common ancestor. A sequence
\langle x_{1}\in\Phi A_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\Phi A_{n}\rangle
is called
diverse

if no two elements are relatives. A natural transformation

\phi\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi
is
diverse

if the corresponding sequence of elements

\langle\phi(A_{i})(1_{A_{i}})\rangle
is.

All the elements of a diverse sequence are different
and more, but not enough more to make the corresponding
transformation monic. One could have
i\neq j

and
f\colon A_{i}\ \>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A,g\colon A_{j}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A
with

\Phi(f)(x_{i})=\Phi(g)(x_{j})
. But if
{\bf A}
is
a groupoid, then
\phi
is monic if and only if it is
diverse. The variables
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
in the
formula we’re abstracting from form a finite discrete
set so diverse restricts to one-one in that case.

Proposition 5.4
.

(1)

\phi
as below is diverse if and only if
for every factorization of
\Phi

\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\scriptstyle{\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{i},-)}

with
(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)\colon\langle C_{1},\dots,C_{m}\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle
in
{\downarrow}{\bf A}
,
we have that
\sigma
is a bijection, i.e.
(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)\in!{\bf A}
.

(2)

Every
\phi
factors as
\psi\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{i},-)
with
(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)\in{\downarrow}{\bf A}

and
\psi
diverse.

Proof.

(1)
\phi
and
\psi
as in the statement correspond
to an
n
-tuple
x_{1}\in A_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\Phi A_{n}

and an
m
-tuple
y_{1}\in\Phi C_{1},\dots,y_{m}\in\Phi C_{m}
,
respectively. The
x
’s and
y
’s are related by

x_{i}=\Phi(f_{i})(y_{\sigma i})\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

If
\sigma
is not one-to-one, say
\sigma(i_{1})=\sigma(i_{2})
, then
x_{i_{1}}
and
x_{i_{2}}
are
relatives as they have the common ancestor

y_{\sigma(i_{1})}=y_{\sigma(i_{2})}
. So the
x_{i}

are not diverse nor is
\phi
.

Conversely, if the
x_{i}
are not diverse, then there
are two
x
’s that are relatives. Assume, for
simplicity of notation, that they are
x_{n-1}

and
x_{n}
. So we have
f\colon C\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A_{n-1},g\colon C\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A_{n}
and
y\in\Phi C
such that

\Phi(f)(y)=x_{n-1}
and
\Phi(g)(y)=x_{n}
.
Then we get a morphism

(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)\colon\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n-2},C\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\langle A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\rangle

\sigma(i)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}i&\mbox{if}&i<n\\
n-1&\mbox{if}&i=n\hbox to0.0pt{\ ,\hss}\end{array}\right.

\langle f_{i}\rangle=\langle 1_{A_{1}},\dots,1_{A_{n-2}},f,g\rangle\hbox to0.0pt{\ .\hss}

Let
\langle y_{1},\dots,y_{n-1}\rangle=\langle x_{1},\dots,x_{n-2},y\rangle
. Then

x_{i}=\Phi(f_{i})(y_{\sigma i})

so the
y
determine a
\psi
giving a factorization as
above, and
\sigma
is not a bijection.

This proves (1).

(2) If
\phi
is not diverse, there exists a factorization
as in (1) with
\sigma
onto but not one-to-one, so

\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)
has fewer terms than
\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)
. If we take, among all factorizations,
one with the minimal number of terms, the
\psi
must
be diverse, otherwise we could factor it again and get
a smaller one.
∎

Corollary 5.3
.

Every equivalence class

\big[x\in F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B),\phi\colon\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Phi\big]

in

\int^{\langle A_{1},\dots,A\rangle\in{\downarrow}{\bf A}}\Delta_{A_{1}}\dots\Delta_{A}[F](0)(B)\times{\bf Set}^{\bf A}\big(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-),\Phi\big)

has a representative in which
\phi
is diverse.

Proof.

Factor
\phi
as in
5.4
(2) above.
Then

\textstyle{y}
\textstyle{\in}
\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-))(B)}
\textstyle{x}
\textstyle{\in}
\textstyle{F(\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-))(B)}

\scriptstyle{F(\sigma,\langle f_{i}\rangle)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(C_{j},-)}
\scriptstyle{\sum_{\sigma}{\bf A}(f_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-)}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{\Phi}

so
[x,\phi]=[y,\psi]
and
\psi
is diverse.
∎

The diverse transformations are our categorified
set injections so

\mbox{Diverse}\ (\sum{\bf A}(A_{i},-),\Phi)

is our version of falling power. Note, however, that
it is not functorial, and we need all transformations
to make it so.
